\displaystyle\geq\frac{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}{2^{18}}\left(\frac{\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0})\tau_{(0)}}{\omega_{d}\mathrm{rch}_{\min}^{d}}\right)^{2/d}\text{ and }\mathop{\mathrm{TV}}(D_{0},D_{1})\leq\tau_{(0)}/2\leq\tau/2.

Summing up the two cases above, for all
\tau\leq 1
we have exhibited some
M_{1}\in\mathcal{M}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}
with properties as above,
\mathop{\mathrm{TV}}(D_{0},D_{1})\leq\tau/2
and

\displaystyle\mathrm{d_{H}}(M_{0},M_{1})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}{2^{18}}\left(\frac{\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0})\min\left\{{\tau,\tau_{(0)}}\right\}}{\omega_{d}\mathrm{rch}_{\min}^{d}}\right)^{2/d}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}{2^{18}}\min\left\{{\frac{1}{2^{22}d^{2}},\left(\frac{\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0})\tau}{\omega_{d}\mathrm{rch}_{\min}^{d}}\right)^{2/d}}\right\},

which concludes the proof.

∎

Applying the technique of
Proposition

H.3
with manifolds
M_{0}
having largest possible volume (typically of order
1/f_{\min}
) in the models
\left\{{0}\right\}\sqcup\mathcal{D}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}({f_{\min}},{f_{\max}},{L})
and
\mathrm{B}(0,R)\sqcap\mathcal{D}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}({f_{\min}},{f_{\max}},{L})
yields the following result.
The proof follows the ideas of
[
AL19
, Lemma 5]
. To our knowledge, the first result of this type dates back to
[
GPPVW12a
, Theorem 6]
.

Lemma H.5
.

•

Assume that
f_{\min}\leq f_{\max}/4
and that

2^{d+1}\sigma_{d}f_{\min}\mathrm{rch}_{\min}^{d}\leq 1.

Then for all
\tau\leq 1
, there exist
D_{0},D_{1}\in\left\{{0}\right\}\sqcup\mathcal{D}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}({f_{\min}},{f_{\max}},{L})
with respective supports
M_{0}
and
M_{1}
such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{d_{H}}(M_{0},M_{1})\geq\frac{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}{2^{20}}\min\left\{{\frac{1}{2^{20}d^{2}},\left(\frac{\tau}{\omega_{d}f_{\min}\mathrm{rch}_{\min}^{d}}\right)^{2/d}}\right\}\text{ and }\mathop{\mathrm{TV}}(D_{0},D_{1})\leq\tau/2.

•

Assume that
\mathrm{rch}_{\min}\leq R/144
and
f_{\min}\leq f_{\max}/96
.
Writing
C_{d}^{\prime}=9(2^{2d+1}\sigma_{d-1})
, assume that

\min_{1\leq k\leq n}\left(\frac{192\mathrm{rch}_{\min}\sqrt{k}}{R}\right)^{k}\leq 2^{d+1}C_{d}^{\prime}f_{\min}\mathrm{rch}_{\min}^{d}\leq 1.

Then for all
\tau\leq 1
, there exist
D_{0},D_{1}\in\mathrm{B}(0,R)\sqcap\mathcal{D}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}({f_{\min}},{f_{\max}},{L})
with respective supports
M_{0}
and
M_{1}
such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{d_{H}}(M_{0},M_{1})\geq\frac{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}{2^{30}}\min\left\{{\frac{1}{2^{10}d^{2}},\left(\frac{\tau}{\omega_{d}f_{\min}\mathrm{rch}_{\min}^{d}}\right)^{2/d}}\right\}\text{ and }\mathop{\mathrm{TV}}(D_{0},D_{1})\leq\tau/2.

Proof of
Lemma

H.5
.

For both models, the idea is to first build a manifold
M_{0}\in\mathcal{M}^{{n},{d}}_{2\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}
with prescribed volume close to
1/f_{\min}
, and then consider the variations of it given by
Proposition

H.3
.

•

Let
M_{0}
be a
d
-dimensional sphere of radius

r_{0}=\left(\frac{1}{2\sigma_{d}f_{\min}}\right)^{1/d}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d+1}\times\left\{{0}\right\}^{n-(d+1)}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
containing
x_{0}=0\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
By construction,
\mathrm{rch}_{M_{0}}=r_{0}\geq 2\mathrm{rch}_{\min}
, so that
M_{0}\in\mathcal{M}^{{n},{d}}_{2\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}
, and one easily checks that
\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0})=1/(2f_{\min})
.
For all
\tau\leq 1
,
Proposition

H.3
asserts that there exists a manifold
M_{1}\in\mathcal{M}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}
such that
x_{0}\in M_{1}
, with volume

1/f_{\max}\leq 1/(4f_{\min})\leq\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0})\leq\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{1})\leq 2\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0})\leq 1/f_{\min},

such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{d_{H}}(M_{0},M_{1})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}{2^{18}}\min\left\{{\frac{1}{2^{22}d^{2}},\left(\frac{\tau}{2\omega_{d}f_{\min}\mathrm{rch}_{\min}^{d}}\right)^{2/d}}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}{2^{20}}\min\left\{{\frac{1}{2^{20}d^{2}},\left(\frac{\tau}{\omega_{d}f_{\min}\mathrm{rch}_{\min}^{d}}\right)^{2/d}}\right\},

and with respective uniform distributions
D_{0}
and
D_{1}
over
M_{0}
and
M_{1}
that satisfy
\mathop{\mathrm{TV}}(D_{0},D_{1})\leq\tau/2
.
Since the densities of
D_{0}
and
D_{1}
are constant and equal to
\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0})^{-1}
and
\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{1})^{-1}
respectively, the bounds on the volumes of
M_{0}
and
M_{1}
show that
D_{0}
and
D_{1}
belong to
\left\{{0}\right\}\sqcup\mathcal{D}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}({f_{\min}},{f_{\max}},{L=0})\subseteq\left\{{0}\right\}\sqcup\mathcal{D}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}({f_{\min}},{f_{\max}},{L})
, which concludes the proof.

•

Let
M_{0}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a submanifold given by
Proposition

H.2
applied with parameters
\mathrm{rch}_{\min}^{\prime}=2\mathrm{rch}_{\min}
,
\mathcal{V}=1/(2f_{\min})
and
R^{\prime}=R/2
.
That is,
M_{0}\in\mathcal{M}^{{n},{d}}_{2\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}
is such that
1/(48f_{\min})\leq\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0})\leq 1/(2f_{\min})
and
M_{0}\subseteq\mathrm{B}(0,R/2)
.
For all
\tau\leq 1
,
Proposition

H.3
asserts that there exists a manifold
M_{1}\in\mathcal{M}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}
such that
\mathrm{d_{H}}(M_{0},M_{1})\leq\mathrm{rch}_{\min}/10
, with volume

1/f_{\max}\leq 1/(96f_{\min})\leq\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0})/2\leq\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{1})\leq 2\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0})\leq 1/f_{\min},

and

\displaystyle\mathrm{d_{H}}(M_{0},M_{1})

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}{2^{18}}\min\left\{{\frac{1}{2^{22}d^{2}},\left(\frac{\tau}{48\omega_{d}f_{\min}\mathrm{rch}_{\min}^{d}}\right)^{2/d}}\right\}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}{2^{30}}\min\left\{{\frac{1}{2^{10}d^{2}},\left(\frac{\tau}{\omega_{d}f_{\min}\mathrm{rch}_{\min}^{d}}\right)^{2/d}}\right\},

and such that the respective uniform distributions
D_{0}
and
D_{1}
over
M_{0}
and
M_{1}
satisfy
\mathop{\mathrm{TV}}(D_{0},D_{1})\leq\tau/2
.
Because
M_{0}\subseteq\mathrm{B}(0,R/2)
and
\mathrm{d_{H}}(M_{0},M_{1})\leq\mathrm{rch}_{\min}/10\leq R/2
, we immediately get that
M_{1}\subseteq\mathrm{B}(0,R/2+R/2)=\mathrm{B}(0,R)
.
As a result, this family clearly provides the existence of the announced
\varepsilon
-packing of

\bigl(\mathrm{B}(0,R)\sqcap\mathcal{M}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}},\mathrm{d_{H}}\bigr)
.
As above, the bounds on the volumes of
M_{0}
and
M_{1}
show that
D_{0},D_{1}\in\mathrm{B}(0,R)\sqcap\mathcal{D}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}({f_{\min}},{f_{\max}},{L=0})\subseteq\mathrm{B}(0,R)\sqcap\mathcal{D}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}({f_{\min}},{f_{\max}},{L})
, which concludes the proof.

∎

H.3.2
Proof of the Informational Lower Bounds for Manifold Estimation

With all the intermediate results above, the proofs of
Theorem

5.2
and
Theorem

5.5
follow straightforwardly.

Proof of
Theorem

5.2
and
Theorem

5.5
.

These are direct applications of
Theorem

G.1
for parameter of interest
\theta(D)=\mathrm{Supp}(D)
and distance
\rho=\mathrm{d_{H}}
, with the hypotheses
D_{0},D_{1}
of the models

\left\{{0}\right\}\sqcup\mathcal{D}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}({f_{\min}},{f_{\max}},{L})

and

\mathrm{B}(0,R)\sqcap\mathcal{D}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}({f_{\min}},{f_{\max}},{L})

given by
Lemma

H.5
.
∎

H.4
Computational Lower Bounds: Packing Number of Manifold Classes

We now prove the computational lower bounds
Theorems

5.3
and
5.6
.
For this, and in order to apply
Theorem

G.2
, we build explicit packings of the manifold classes.
To study the two models and the different regimes of parameters, we exhibit two types of such packings.
The first ones that we describe (
Proposition

H.4
) use translations of a fixed manifold
M_{0}
in the ambient space, and are called ambient packings (see
Section

H.4.1
).
The second ones (
Proposition

H.5
) use a local smooth bumping strategy based on a fixed manifold
M_{0}
, and are called intrinsic packings (see
Section

H.4.2
).
Finally, the proof of the computational lower bounds are presented in
Section

H.4.3
.

H.4.1
Global Ambient Packings

To derive the first manifold packing lower bound, we will use translations in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
and the following lemma.

Lemma H.6
.

La
K
be a compact subset of
\mathbb{R}^{n}
. Given
v\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, let
K_{v}=\left\{{p+v,p\in K}\right\}
be the translation of
K
by the vector
v
. Then
\mathrm{d_{H}}(K,K_{v})=\left\|v\right\|
.

Proof of
Lemma

H.6
.

If
v=0
, the result is straightforward, so let us assume that
v\neq 0
.
Since
K
is compact, the map
g
defined for
p\in K
by
g(p)=\left\langle{v/\left\|v\right\|},{p}\right\rangle
attains its maximum at some
p_{0}\in K
.
But by definition of
K_{v}
,
p_{0}+v\in K_{v}
, so

\displaystyle\mathrm{d_{H}}(K,K_{v})

\displaystyle\geq\mathrm{d}(p_{0}+v,K)

\displaystyle=\min_{p\in K}\left\|(p_{0}+v)-p\right\|

\displaystyle\geq\min_{p\in K}\left\langle{\frac{v}{\left\|v\right\|}},{(p_{0}+v)-p}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\|v\right\|+\min_{p\in K}\left\langle{\frac{v}{\left\|v\right\|}},{p_{0}-p}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\left\|v\right\|.

On the other hand, for all
p\in K
we have
p+v\in K_{v}
, yielding
\mathrm{d}(p,K_{v})\leq\left\|v\right\|
, and symmetrically
\mathrm{d}(p+v,K)\leq\left\|v\right\|
. Therefore
\mathrm{d_{H}}(K,K_{v})\leq\left\|v\right\|
, which concludes the proof.
∎

As a result, packings of sets in
\mathbb{R}^{n}
naturally yields packings in the manifold space, by translating a fixed manifold
M_{0}\subset\mathbb{R}^{n}
. With this remark in mind, we get the following ambient packing lower bound.

Proposition H.4
.

Assume that
\mathrm{rch}_{\min}\leq R/24
.
Writing
C_{d}=9(2^{d}\sigma_{d-1})
, let
\mathcal{V}>0
be such that

1\leq\frac{\mathcal{V}}{C_{d}\mathrm{rch}_{\min}^{d}}\leq\max_{1\leq k\leq n}\left(\frac{R}{48\mathrm{rch}_{\min}\sqrt{k}}\right)^{k}.

Then for all
\varepsilon\leq R/2
,

\displaystyle\log\mathrm{pk}_{\bigl(\mathrm{B}(0,R)\sqcap\mathcal{M}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}},\mathrm{d_{H}}\bigr)}(\varepsilon)\geq n\log\left(\frac{R}{4\varepsilon}\right),

and such a packing can be chosen so that all its elements
M

have volume
\mathcal{V}/6\leq\mathcal{H}^{d}(M)\leq\mathcal{V}
.

Proof of
Proposition

H.4
.

Let
z_{1},\dots,z_{N}\in\mathrm{B}(0,R/2)
be a
r
-packing of
\mathrm{B}(0,R/2)
. From

Proposition

B.4
, such a packing can be taken so
that
N\geq(R/(4r))^{n}
. Applying
Proposition

H.2

with parameters
\mathrm{rch}_{\min}
,
\mathcal{V}
and
R^{\prime}=R/2
, we get the
existence of some
M_{0}\in\mathcal{M}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}
such that

\mathcal{V}/6\leq\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0})\leq\mathcal{V}
and
M_{0}\subseteq\mathrm{B}(0,R/2)
.
Note that for all
z\in\mathrm{B}(0,R/2)
, the translation

M_{z}=\left\{{p+z,p\in M_{0}}\right\}
belongs to
\mathcal{M}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}
,
has the same volume as
M_{0}
, and satisfies

M_{z}\subseteq\mathrm{B}(0,R/2+\left\|z\right\|)\subseteq\mathrm{B}(0,R)
.
In addition,
Lemma

H.6
asserts
that for all
z,z^{\prime}\in\mathrm{B}(0,R/2)
,
\mathrm{d_{H}}(M_{z},M_{z^{\prime}})=\left\|z-z^{\prime}\right\|
.
In particular, for all

i\neq j\in\left\{{1,\dots,N}\right\}
,
\mathrm{d_{H}}(M_{z_{i}},M_{z_{j}})=\left\|z_{i}-z_{j}\right\|>2r
.
As a result, the family
\left\{{M_{z_{i}}}\right\}_{1\leq i\leq N}
provides us with an

r
-packing of
\bigl(\mathrm{B}(0,R)\sqcap\mathcal{M}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}},\mathrm{d_{H}}\bigr)

with cardinality
N\geq(R/(4r))^{n}
, and composed of submanifold with volume

\mathcal{V}/6\leq\mathcal{H}^{d}(M)\leq\mathcal{V}
, which concludes the proof.
∎

H.4.2
Local Intrinsic Packings

In the same spirit as
Proposition

H.3
for
informational lower bounds, the following result allows to build packings of
manifold classes by small perturbations of a base submanifold
M_{0}
. Note, again,
that the larger the volume
\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0})
, the stronger the result.

Proposition H.5
.

For all
M_{0}\in\mathcal{M}^{{n},{d}}_{2\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}
and
r\leq\mathrm{rch}_{\min}/(2^{34}d^{2})
,
there exists a family of submanifolds

\left\{{M_{s}}\right\}_{1\leq s\leq\mathcal{N}}\subseteq\mathcal{M}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}
with
cardinality
\mathcal{N}
such that

\log\mathcal{N}\geq n\frac{\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0})}{\omega_{d}\mathrm{rch}_{\min}^{d}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}{2^{19}r}\right)^{d/2},

and that satisfies:

•

M_{0}
and
\left\{{M_{s}}\right\}_{1\leq s\leq\mathcal{N}}
have a point in common:

M_{0}\cap\bigl(\cap_{1\leq s\leq\mathcal{N}}M_{s}\bigr)\neq\emptyset
.

•

For all
s\in\left\{{1,\dots,\mathcal{N}}\right\}
,

\mathrm{d_{H}}(M_{0},M_{s})\leq 23r\text{ and }\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0})/2\leq\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{s})\leq 2\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0}).

•

For all
s\neq s^{\prime}\in\left\{{1,\dots,\mathcal{N}}\right\}

\mathrm{d_{H}}(M_{s},M_{s^{\prime}})>2r
.

Proof of
Proposition

H.5
.

For
\delta\leq\mathrm{rch}_{\min}/8
to be chosen later, let
\left\{{p_{i}}\right\}_{1\leq i\leq N}
be a maximal
\delta
-packing of
M_{0}
.
From
Proposition

B.3
, this maximal packing has cardinality
N\geq\frac{\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0})}{\omega_{d}(4\delta)^{d}}
.

Let
\eta>0
be a parameter to be chosen later.
Given a family of unit vectors
\mathbf{w}=(w_{i})_{1\leq i\leq N}\in\left(\mathbb{R}^{n}\right)^{N}
normal at the
p_{i}
’s, i.e.
w_{i}\in\left(T_{p_{i}}M\right)^{\perp}
and
\left\|w_{i}\right\|=1
, we let
\Phi_{\mathbf{w}}
be the function defined in
Lemma

H.1
, that maps any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
to

\displaystyle\Phi_{\mathbf{w}}(x)

\displaystyle=x+\eta\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\phi\left(\frac{x-p_{i}}{\delta}\right)w_{i}\right),

where
\phi:\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
is the real bump function

\phi(y)=\exp\left(-{\left\|y\right\|^{2}}/{(1-\left\|y\right\|^{2})}\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\mathrm{B}(0,1)}(y)

of
Lemma

H.1
.
We let
M_{\mathbf{w}}=\Phi_{\mathbf{w}}(M_{0})
be the image of
M_{0}
by
\Phi_{\mathbf{w}}
. The set
M_{\mathbf{w}}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
hence coincides with
M_{0}
, except in the
\delta
-neighborhoods of the
p_{i}
’s, where it has a bump of size
\eta
towards direction
w_{i}
.
Note by now that up to rotations of its coordinates, the vector
\mathbf{w}=(w_{i})_{1\leq i\leq N}
belongs to
\mathcal{S}^{n-d}(0,1)^{N}
.
Combining
Proposition

H.1
and
Lemma

H.1
, we see that
M_{\mathbf{w}}\in\mathcal{M}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}
and
\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0})/2\leq\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{\mathbf{w}})\leq 2\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0})
as soon as

\displaystyle\frac{5\eta}{2\delta}\leq\frac{1}{10d}\text{ and }\frac{23\eta}{\delta^{2}}\leq\frac{1}{4\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}.

In the rest of the proof, we will work with these two inequalities holding true.
In particular, because
\left\|\Phi_{\mathbf{w}}-I_{n}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\eta
, we immediately get that
\mathrm{d_{H}}(M_{0},M_{\mathbf{w}})\leq\eta
.
We note also that all the
\Phi_{\mathbf{w}}
’s coincide with the identity map on (say)
M_{0}\cap\partial\mathrm{B}(p_{1},\delta)
, so that
M_{0}\cap\bigl(\cap_{\mathbf{w}}M_{\mathbf{w}}\bigr)
contains
M_{0}\cap\partial\mathrm{B}(x_{1},\delta)
and is hence non-empty.

We now take two different families of unit normal vectors
\mathbf{w}
and
\mathbf{w}^{\prime}
(i.e.
w_{i},w_{i}^{\prime}\in\left(T_{p_{i}}M_{0}\right)^{\perp}
and
\left\|w_{i}\right\|=\left\|w_{i}^{\prime}\right\|=1
for
1\leq i\leq N
), and we will show that their associated submanifolds
M_{\mathbf{w}}
and
M_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}
are far away in Hausdorff distance as soon as
\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\left\|w_{i}-w_{i}^{\prime}\right\|
is large enough. To this aim, we first see that by construction,
\Phi_{\mathbf{w}}(p_{i})=p_{i}+\eta w_{i}\in\Phi_{\mathbf{w}}(M_{0})=M_{\mathbf{w}}
for all
i\in\left\{{1,\dots,N}\right\}
. In particular,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d_{H}}(M_{\mathbf{w}},M_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\geq\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\mathrm{d}(p_{i}+\eta w_{i},M_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}).

Let us fix a free parameter
\lambda_{i}\in[0,1]
to be chosen later. As
\left\|\Phi_{\mathbf{w^{\prime}}}-I_{n}\right\|_{\infty}\leq\eta
, we can write for all
i\in\left\{{1,\dots,N}\right\}
that

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}(p_{i}+\eta w_{i},M_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\left(p_{i}+\eta w_{i},\Phi_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(M_{0})\right)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}\left(p_{i}+\eta w_{i},\Phi_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(M_{0}\setminus\mathrm{B}(p_{i},\lambda_{i}\delta))\right)\wedge\mathrm{d}\bigl(p_{i}+\eta w_{i},\Phi_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(M_{0}\cap\mathrm{B}(p_{i},\lambda_{i}\delta))\bigr)

\displaystyle\geq(\lambda_{i}\delta-\eta)\wedge\mathrm{d}\bigl(p_{i}+\eta w_{i},\Phi_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(M_{0}\cap\mathrm{B}(p_{i},\lambda_{i}\delta))\bigr).

Further investigating the term

\mathrm{d}\left(p_{i}+\eta w_{i},\Phi_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(M_{0}\cap\mathrm{B}(p_{i},\lambda_{i}\delta))\right)
,
we see that for all
x\in M_{0}\cap\mathrm{B}(p_{i},\lambda_{i}\delta)\subseteq\mathrm{B}(p_{i},\delta)
,

\Phi_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(x)=x+\eta\phi\left(\frac{x-p_{i}}{\delta}\right)w_{i}
.
But from
[
Fed59
, Theorem 4.18]
,
\mathrm{rch}_{M_{0}}\geq 2\mathrm{rch}_{\min}
ensures that any
x\in M_{0}\cap\mathrm{B}(p_{i},\lambda_{i}\delta)
can be written as
x=p_{i}+v+u
, where
v\in T_{p_{i}}M_{0}
with
\left\|v\right\|\leq\lambda_{i}\delta
, and
u\in\bigl(T_{p_{i}}M_{0}\bigr)^{\perp}
with
\left\|u\right\|\leq(\lambda_{i}\delta)^{2}/(4\mathrm{rch}_{\min})
.
As a result, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}

\displaystyle\left(p_{i}+\eta w_{i},\Phi_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(M_{0}\cap\mathrm{B}(p_{i},\lambda_{i}\delta))\right)

\displaystyle\geq\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}v\in T_{p_{i}}M_{0},\left\|v\right\|\leq\lambda_{i}\delta\\
u\in(T_{p_{i}}M_{0})^{\perp},\left\|u\right\|\leq(\lambda_{i}\delta)^{2}/(4\mathrm{rch}_{\min})\end{subarray}}\left\|v+u+\eta\left(\phi\left(\frac{v+u}{\delta}\right)w^{\prime}_{i}-w_{i}\right)\right\|.

But in the above minimum,
v
is orthogonal to
u,w_{i}
and
w_{i}^{\prime}
, so

\displaystyle\left\|v+u+\eta\left(\phi\left(\frac{v+u}{\delta}\right)w^{\prime}_{i}-w_{i}\right)\right\|\geq\left\|u+\eta\left(\phi\left(\frac{v+u}{\delta}\right)w^{\prime}_{i}-w_{i}\right)\right\|.

Additionally,
\phi\left(\frac{v+u}{\delta}\right)
ranges in (a subset of)
[0,1]
since
0\leq\phi\leq 1
.
In particular,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\left(p_{i}+\eta w_{i},\Phi_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(M_{0}\cap\mathrm{B}(p_{i},\lambda_{i}\delta))\right)

\displaystyle\geq\min_{\begin{subarray}{c}u\in(T_{p_{i}}M_{0})^{\perp},\left\|u\right\|\leq(\lambda_{i}\delta)^{2}/(4\mathrm{rch}_{\min})\\
0\leq t\leq 1\end{subarray}}\left\|u+\eta\left(tw^{\prime}_{i}-w_{i}\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\geq\min_{0\leq t\leq 1}\eta\left\|tw^{\prime}_{i}-w_{i}\right\|-\frac{(\lambda_{i}\delta)^{2}}{4\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}

\displaystyle=\eta\left\|\left(0\vee\left\langle{w_{i}},{w^{\prime}_{i}}\right\rangle\right)w^{\prime}_{i}-w_{i}\right\|-\frac{(\lambda_{i}\delta)^{2}}{4\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}

\displaystyle\geq\eta\frac{\left\|w^{\prime}_{i}-w_{i}\right\|}{2}-\frac{(\lambda_{i}\delta)^{2}}{4\mathrm{rch}_{\min}},

where the second line follows from triangle inequality, and the last two from elementary calculations.
Putting everything together, we have shown that for all
\lambda_{1},\dots,\lambda_{N}\in[0,1]
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d_{H}}(M_{\mathbf{w}},M_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\geq\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\left\{{(\lambda_{i}\delta-\eta)\wedge\left(\eta\frac{\left\|w^{\prime}_{i}-w_{i}\right\|}{2}-\frac{(\lambda_{i}\delta)^{2}}{4\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}\right)}\right\}.

One easily checks that under the above assumptions on the parameters,

\lambda_{i}:=\frac{\sqrt{\sqrt{2}\mathrm{rch}_{\min}\left\|w^{\prime}_{i}-w_{i}\right\|\eta}}{\delta}

provides valid choices of
\lambda_{i}\in[0,1]
. Plugging these values in the previous bound yields

\displaystyle\mathrm{d_{H}}(M_{\mathbf{w}},M_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\geq\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\left\{{\left(\sqrt{\sqrt{2}\mathrm{rch}_{\min}\left\|w^{\prime}_{i}-w_{i}\right\|\eta}-\eta\right)\wedge\left(\eta\frac{\left\|w^{\prime}_{i}-w_{i}\right\|}{8}\right)}\right\},

so that if we further assume that
\left\|w^{\prime}_{i}-w_{i}\right\|\geq 4\sqrt{2}\eta/\mathrm{rch}_{\min}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathrm{d_{H}}(M_{\mathbf{w}},M_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\geq\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\left\{{\eta\wedge\left(\eta\frac{\left\|w^{\prime}_{i}-w_{i}\right\|}{8}\right)}\right\}

\displaystyle=\frac{\eta}{8}\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\left\|w^{\prime}_{i}-w_{i}\right\|,

where the last line follows from
\left\|w_{i}-w^{\prime}_{i}\right\|\leq\left\|w_{i}\right\|+\left\|w^{\prime}_{i}\right\|\leq 2
.

Setting
\eta=\delta^{2}/(92\mathrm{rch}_{\min})
, which is a value that satisfies all the requirements above as soon as
\delta\leq\mathrm{rch}_{\min}/(2300d)
, we have built a family of submanifolds
\left\{{M_{\mathbf{w}}}\right\}_{\mathbf{w}}
of
\mathcal{M}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}
indexed by
\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{S}^{n-d}(0,1)^{N}
, such that
\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0})/2\leq\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{\mathbf{w}})\leq 2\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0})
, and which are guaranteed to satisfy

\displaystyle\mathrm{d_{H}}(M_{\mathbf{w}},M_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}})

\displaystyle>\frac{\delta^{2}}{8(92\mathrm{rch}_{\min})}\times\frac{1}{4}\geq 2\left(\frac{\delta^{2}}{2082\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}\right),

provided that
\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\left\|w^{\prime}_{i}-w_{i}\right\|>1/4=2/8
.
As a result, if we consider
(1/8)
-packings of the unit spheres
\mathcal{S}_{(T_{p_{i}}M_{0})^{\perp}}(0,1)=\mathcal{S}^{n-d}(0,1)
for
i\in\left\{{1,\dots,N}\right\}
, then for all
\delta\leq\mathrm{rch}_{\min}/(2300d)
, it naturally defines a
\left(\frac{\delta^{2}}{2082\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}\right)
-packing of
\mathcal{M}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}
with cardinality
\mathcal{N}
a least

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}

\displaystyle\geq\mathrm{pk}_{\mathcal{S}^{n-d}(0,1)}(1/8)^{N}\geq\mathrm{pk}_{\mathcal{S}^{n-d}(0,1)}(1/8)^{\frac{\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0})}{\omega_{d}(4\delta)^{d}}},

and which consists of elements
M_{\mathbf{w}}
such that
\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0})/2\leq\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{\mathbf{w}})\leq 2\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0})
and
\mathrm{d_{H}}(M_{0},M_{\mathbf{w}})\leq\eta=\delta^{2}/(92\mathrm{rch}_{\min})
.
In particular, by setting
r=\frac{\delta^{2}}{2082\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}
, then for all
0<r\leq\mathrm{rch}_{\min}/(2^{34}d^{2})
, we have exhibited a
r
-packing of
\mathcal{M}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}
of cardinality
\mathcal{N}
with

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0})}{\omega_{d}(4\sqrt{2082\mathrm{rch}_{\min}r})^{d}}\log\mathrm{pk}_{\mathcal{S}^{n-d}(0,1)}(1/8),

composed of submanifolds having volume as above, and
\mathrm{d_{H}}(M_{0},M_{\mathbf{w}})\leq 2082r/92\leq 23r
.
From
Proposition

B.4
,

\log\mathrm{pk}_{\mathcal{S}^{n-d}(0,1)}(1/8)\geq(n-d)\log 2
.
Finally, by considering the cases
d\leq n/2
and
d\geq n/2
, one easily checks that
(n-d)\geq n/(2d)
.
In all, we obtain the announced bound

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0})}{\omega_{d}(4\sqrt{2082\mathrm{rch}_{\min}r})^{d}}\frac{n\log 2}{2d}

\displaystyle\geq n\frac{\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0})}{\omega_{d}\mathrm{rch}_{\min}^{d}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}{2^{19}r}\right)^{d/2},

which yields the announced result.
∎

Applying the technique of
Proposition

H.5
with manifolds
M_{0}
having a large prescribed volume
\left\{{0}\right\}\sqcup\mathcal{M}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}
and
\mathrm{B}(0,R)\sqcap\mathcal{M}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}
respectively yields the following result.

Proposition H.6
.

Let
\mathcal{V}>0
and
\varepsilon\leq\mathrm{rch}_{\min}/(2^{34}d^{2})
.

•

Assume that

1\leq\frac{\mathcal{V}}{2^{d+1}\sigma_{d}\mathrm{rch}_{\min}^{d}}.

Then,

\displaystyle\log\mathrm{pk}_{\bigl(\left\{{0}\right\}\sqcup\mathcal{M}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}},\mathrm{d_{H}}\bigr)}(\varepsilon)\geq n\frac{\mathcal{V}}{\omega_{d}\mathrm{rch}_{\min}^{d}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}{2^{21}\varepsilon}\right)^{d/2}.

Furthermore, this packing can be chosen so that all its elements
M
satisfy

\mathcal{V}/4\leq\mathcal{H}^{d}(M)\leq\mathcal{V}.

•

Assume that
\mathrm{rch}_{\min}\leq R/144
. Writing
C_{d}^{\prime}=9(2^{2d+1}\sigma_{d-1})
, assume that

1\leq\frac{\mathcal{V}}{2^{d+1}C_{d}^{\prime}\mathrm{rch}_{\min}^{d}}\leq\max_{1\leq k\leq n}\left(\frac{R}{192\mathrm{rch}_{\min}\sqrt{k}}\right)^{k}.

Then,

\displaystyle\log\mathrm{pk}_{\bigl(\mathrm{B}(0,R)\sqcap\mathcal{M}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}},\mathrm{d_{H}}\bigr)}(\varepsilon)\geq n\frac{\mathcal{V}}{\omega_{d}\mathrm{rch}_{\min}^{d}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}{2^{31}\varepsilon}\right)^{d/2}.

Furthermore, this packing can be chosen so that all its elements
M
satisfy

\mathcal{V}/96\leq\mathcal{H}^{d}(M)\leq\mathcal{V}.

Proof of
Proposition

H.6
.

For both models, the idea is to first build a manifold
M_{0}\in\mathcal{M}^{{n},{d}}_{2\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}
with prescribed volume close to
\mathcal{V}
, and then consider the variations of it given by
Proposition

H.5
.

•

Let
M_{0}
be the centered
d
-dimensional sphere of radius

r_{0}=\left(\frac{\mathcal{V}/2}{\sigma_{d}}\right)^{1/d}
in
\mathbb{R}^{d+1}\times\left\{{0}\right\}^{n-(d+1)}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
By construction,
\mathrm{rch}_{M_{0}}=r_{0}\geq 2\mathrm{rch}_{\min}
, so that
M_{0}\in\mathcal{M}^{{n},{d}}_{2\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}
. Furthermore, one easily checks that
\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0})=\mathcal{V}/2
.
From
Proposition

H.5
, there exists a family of submanifolds
\left\{{M_{s}}\right\}_{1\leq s\leq\mathcal{N}}\subseteq\mathcal{M}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}
with cardinality
\mathcal{N}
such that

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}

\displaystyle\geq n\frac{\mathcal{V}/2}{\omega_{d}\mathrm{rch}_{\min}^{d}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}{2^{19}\varepsilon}\right)^{d/2}

\displaystyle\geq n\frac{\mathcal{V}}{\omega_{d}\mathrm{rch}_{\min}^{d}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}{2^{21}\varepsilon}\right)^{d/2},

that all share a point
x_{0}\in\cap_{1\leq s\leq\mathcal{N}}M_{s}
, and such that

\mathrm{d_{H}}(M_{s},M_{s^{\prime}})>2\varepsilon
for all
s\neq s^{\prime}\in\left\{{1,\dots,\mathcal{N}}\right\}
,
with volumes

\mathcal{V}/4=\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0})/2\leq\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{s})\leq 2\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0})=\mathcal{V}
.
As a result, the family given by the translations
M^{\prime}_{s}=M_{s}-x_{0}
clearly provides the existence of the announced
\varepsilon
-packing of

\bigl(\left\{{0}\right\}\sqcup\mathcal{M}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}},\mathrm{d_{H}}\bigr)
.

•

Let
M_{0}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{n}
be a submanifold given by
Proposition

H.2
applied with parameters
\mathrm{rch}_{\min}^{\prime}=2\mathrm{rch}_{\min}
,
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}=\mathcal{V}/2
and
R^{\prime}=R/2
. That is,
M_{0}\in\mathcal{M}^{{n},{d}}_{2\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}
is such that
\mathcal{V}/48\leq\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0})\leq\mathcal{V}/2
and
M_{0}\subseteq\mathrm{B}(0,R/2)
.
From
Proposition

H.5
, there exists a family of submanifolds
\left\{{M_{s}}\right\}_{1\leq s\leq\mathcal{N}}\subseteq\mathcal{M}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}
with cardinality
\mathcal{N}
such that

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}

\displaystyle\geq n\frac{\mathcal{V}/48}{\omega_{d}\mathrm{rch}_{\min}^{d}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}{2^{19}\varepsilon}\right)^{d/2}

\displaystyle\geq n\frac{\mathcal{V}}{\omega_{d}\mathrm{rch}_{\min}^{d}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}{2^{31}\varepsilon}\right)^{d/2},

with

\mathrm{d_{H}}(M_{0},M_{s})\leq 23\varepsilon

and
\mathrm{d_{H}}(M_{s},M_{s^{\prime}})>2\varepsilon
for all
s\neq s^{\prime}\in\left\{{1,\dots,\mathcal{N}}\right\}
,
and volumes

\mathcal{V}/96\leq\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0})/2\leq\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{s})\leq 2\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{0})\leq\mathcal{V}
.
Because
M_{0}\subseteq\mathrm{B}(0,R/2)
and
\mathrm{d_{H}}(M_{0},M_{s})\leq 23\varepsilon
for all
s\in\left\{{1,\dots,\mathcal{N}}\right\}
, we immediately get that
M_{s}\subseteq\mathrm{B}(0,R/2+23\varepsilon)\subseteq\mathrm{B}(0,R)
.
As a result, this family clearly provides the existence of the announced
\varepsilon
-packing of

\bigl(\mathrm{B}(0,R)\sqcap\mathcal{M}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}},\mathrm{d_{H}}\bigr)
.

∎

H.4.3
Proof of the Computational Lower Bounds for Manifold Estimation

We are now in position to prove the computational lower bounds presented in this work.
First, we turn to the infeasibiliy result of manifold estimation using statistical queries in the unbounded model
\mathcal{D}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}({f_{\min}},{f_{\max}},{L})
(
Proposition

2.1
).

Proof of
Proposition

2.1
.

Since
\sigma_{d}f_{\min}\mathrm{rch}_{\min}^{d}\leq 1
, the uniform probability distribution
D_{0}
over the centered unit
d
-sphere
M_{0}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d+1}\times\left\{{0}\right\}^{n-(d+1)}
of radius
\mathrm{rch}_{\min}
belongs to

\mathcal{D}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}({f_{\min}},{f_{\max}},{L})
.
Given a unit vector
v\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, the invariance of the model by translation yields that the uniform distributions
D_{k}
over
M_{k}=\left\{{p+(3k\varepsilon)v,p\in M_{0}}\right\}
, for
k\in\mathbb{Z}
, also belong to
\mathcal{D}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}({f_{\min}},{f_{\max}},{L})
.
But for all
k\neq k^{\prime}\in\mathbb{Z}
,
\mathrm{d_{H}}(M_{k},M_{k^{\prime}})=3|k-k^{\prime}|\varepsilon>2\varepsilon
.
Hence, writing

\mathcal{M}=\left\{{\mathrm{Supp}(D),D\in\mathcal{D}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}({f_{\min}},{f_{\max}},{L})}\right\},

we see that the family
\left\{{M_{k}}\right\}_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}
forms an infinite
\varepsilon
-packing of
(\mathcal{M},\mathrm{d_{H}})
.
From
Theorem

G.2
, we get that the statistical query complexity of manifold estimation over the model
\mathcal{D}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}({f_{\min}},{f_{\max}},{L})
with precision
\varepsilon
is infinite, which concludes the proof.
∎

We finally come to the proofs of the computational lower bounds over the fixed point model
\left\{{0}\right\}\sqcup\mathcal{D}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}({f_{\min}},{f_{\max}},{L})
(
Theorem

5.3
) and the bounding ball model
\mathrm{B}(0,R)\sqcap\mathcal{D}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}({f_{\min}},{f_{\max}},{L})
(
Theorem

5.6
).

Proof of
Theorem

5.3
and
Theorem

5.6
.

For both results, the idea is to exhibit large enough
\varepsilon
-packings of
\mathcal{M}=\left\{{\mathrm{Supp}(D),D\in\mathcal{D}}\right\}
, and apply
Theorem

G.2
. In each case, the assumptions on the parameters
f_{\min},f_{\max}
,
\mathrm{rch}_{\min}
and
d
ensure that the uniform distributions over the manifolds given by the packings of
Proposition

H.6
(and
Proposition

H.4
for
Theorem

5.6
) applied with
\mathcal{V}=1/f_{\min}
belong to the model, and hence that
\mathcal{M}
contain these packings.

•

To prove
Theorem

5.3
, let us write

\mathcal{M}_{0}:=\left\{{\mathrm{Supp}(D),D\in\left\{{0}\right\}\sqcup\mathcal{D}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}({f_{\min}},{f_{\max}},{L})}\right\}\subseteq\left\{{0}\right\}\sqcup\mathcal{M}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}.

From
Theorem

G.2
, any randomized SQ algorithm estimating
M=\mathrm{Supp}(D)
over the model
\left\{{0}\right\}\sqcup\mathcal{D}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}({f_{\min}},{f_{\max}},{L})
with precision
\varepsilon
and with probability of success at least
1-\alpha
must make at least

q\geq\frac{\log\bigl((1-\alpha)\mathrm{pk}_{(\mathcal{M}_{0},\mathrm{d_{H}})}(\varepsilon)\bigr)}{\log(1+\left\lfloor 1/\tau\right\rfloor)}

queries to
\mathrm{STAT}(\tau)
.
Furthermore, let
\left\{{M_{i}}\right\}_{1\leq i\leq\mathcal{N}}
be an
\varepsilon
-packing of
\left\{{0}\right\}\sqcup\mathcal{M}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}
given by
Proposition

H.6
, that we apply with volume
\mathcal{V}=1/f_{\min}
.
Recall that these manifolds are guaranteed to have volumes
1/(4f_{\min})\leq\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{i})\leq 1/f_{\min}
.
From the assumptions on the parameters of the model, we get that the uniform distributions
\left\{{D_{i}:=\mathbbm{1}_{M_{i}}\mathcal{H}^{d}/\mathcal{H}^{d}(M_{i})}\right\}_{1\leq i\leq\mathcal{N}}
over the
M_{i}
’s all belong to
\left\{{0}\right\}\sqcup\mathcal{D}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}({f_{\min}},{f_{\max}},{L})
.
In particular, the family
\left\{{M_{i}}\right\}_{1\leq i\leq\mathcal{N}}
is also an
\varepsilon
-packing of
\mathcal{M}_{0}
, and therefore

\displaystyle\log\bigl(\mathrm{pk}_{(\mathcal{M}_{0},\mathrm{d_{H}})}(\varepsilon)\bigr)

\displaystyle\geq\log\mathcal{N}\geq n\frac{1}{\omega_{d}f_{\min}\mathrm{rch}_{\min}^{d}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}{2^{21}\varepsilon}\right)^{d/2},

which yields the announced result.

•

Similarly, to prove
Theorem

5.6
, write

\mathcal{M}_{R}:=\left\{{\mathrm{Supp}(D),D\in\mathrm{B}(0,R)\sqcap\mathcal{D}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}({f_{\min}},{f_{\max}},{L})}\right\}\subseteq\mathrm{B}(0,R)\sqcap\mathcal{M}^{{n},{d}}_{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}},

and apply
Theorem

G.2
to get

q\geq\frac{\log\bigl((1-\alpha)\mathrm{pk}_{(\mathcal{M}_{R},\mathrm{d_{H}})}(\varepsilon)\bigr)}{\log(1+\left\lfloor 1/\tau\right\rfloor)}.

The assumptions on the parameters ensure that the packings exhibited in
Proposition

H.4
and
Proposition

H.6
applied with volume
\mathcal{V}=1/f_{\min}
are included in
\mathcal{M}_{R}
, so that

\displaystyle\log\bigl(\mathrm{pk}_{(\mathcal{M}_{R},\mathrm{d_{H}})}(\varepsilon)\bigr)

\displaystyle\geq n\max\left\{{\log\left(\frac{R}{4\varepsilon}\right),\frac{1}{\omega_{d}f_{\min}\mathrm{rch}_{\min}^{d}}\left(\frac{\mathrm{rch}_{\min}}{2^{31}\varepsilon}\right)^{d/2}}\right\},

which concludes the proof.

∎
